905	def _dispatch_attested(
906	    *,
907	    slug: str,
908	    command: str,
909	    step: AttestedStep,
910	    path_str: str,
911	    path_tuple: tuple[str, ...],
912	    events_path: Path,
913	    run_id: str,
914	    run_started_actor: str | None,
915	    project_root: Path,
916	    iteration: int | None = None,
917	    item_id: str | None = None,
918	) -> GateDecision:
919	    matched, args = match_attested_command(command, step.command)
920	    if not matched:
921	        _reject(slug, "incoming command does not match plan[cursor]", abort=False)
922	
923	    attestor_kind, attestor_id = validate_attested_identity(
924	        slug=slug,
925	        step=step,
926	        args=args,
927	        run_started_actor=run_started_actor,
928	    )
929	
930	    if item_id is not None:
931	        event = make_item_attested_event(
932	            path_tuple,
933	            item_id,
934	            attestor_kind=attestor_kind,
935	            attestor_id=attestor_id,
936	            evidence=args.evidence,
937	        )
938	    else:
939	        event = make_step_attested_event(path_str, attestor_kind, attestor_id, args.evidence)
940	    if is_author_test_mode():
941	        event["source"] = "author_test"
942	    append_event(events_path, event)
943	    decision = GateDecision(
944	        active=True,
945	        run_id=run_id,
946	        plan_step_id=path_str,
947	        events_path=events_path,
948	        reentry=False,
949	        step_kind="attested",
950	        slug=slug,
951	        plan_step_path=path_tuple,
952	        produces=step.produces,
953	        project_root=project_root,
954	        iteration=iteration,
955	        item_id=item_id,
956	    )
957	    if step.produces:
958	        _run_inline_checks(decision, step.produces)
959	    if iteration is not None:
960	        feedback = _extract_iterate_feedback(args.evidence)
961	        if feedback is not None:
962	            write_iteration_feedback(decision, feedback)
963	            append_event(
964	                events_path,
965	                make_iteration_failed_event(
966	                    path_tuple,
967	                    iteration,
968	                    reason="iterate_feedback",
969	                ),
970	            )
971	    return decision
972	
973	
974	def _extract_iterate_feedback(evidence: tuple[str, ...]) -> str | None:
975	    for item in evidence:
976	        if item.startswith(ITERATE_FEEDBACK_PREFIX):
977	            return item[len(ITERATE_FEEDBACK_PREFIX):]
978	    return None
979	
980	
981	def write_iteration_feedback(decision: GateDecision, feedback: str) -> None:
982	    if (
983	        decision.slug is None
984	        or decision.run_id is None
985	        or decision.iteration is None
986	        or decision.project_root is None
987	        or not decision.plan_step_path
988	    ):
989	        return
990	    iter_dir = step_dir_for_path(
991	        decision.slug,
992	        decision.run_id,
993	        decision.plan_step_path,
994	        iteration=decision.iteration,
995	        root=decision.project_root.parent,
996	    )
997	    iter_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
998	    feedback_path = iter_dir / "feedback.json"
999	    cumulative: list[str] = []

```
40	- **Step** — the unit of work inside a run. One type, with `requires_ack`, `assignee`, `produces`, optional `command` (leaf) or `children` (group).
41	- **Session** — per-tab binding to (project, timeline, run). Ephemeral, resumable. Multiple agents can share via read-only attach + explicit takeover.
42	
43	### Current state in one paragraph
44	
45	Project, run, and the step kernel (`plan.json`, `events.jsonl`, `produces/`) all exist. Timeline is **not** a container — it's just a per-run output artifact. Sessions don't exist; `<project>/active_run.json` is project-global on disk and races between tabs. The thread system competes with `active_run.json` and the implicit cwd-derived project — three "active" pointers, none canonical. SKILL.md / AGENTS.md predate the task framework and don't teach `astrid status` / `next` / `ack`. Canonical orchestrators (`builtin.hype` etc.) don't emit `plan.json`. The step model is overcomplicated (`code` vs `attested`, `AckRule` `agent` vs `actor`, separate `nested` kind, separate executor/orchestrator abstraction).
46	
47	### Load-bearing decisions (apply across all sprints)
48	
49	- **ULIDs are identity, slugs are aliases** for every entity (project, timeline, run, session, step). Slugs are mutable display; ULIDs never change. Old references stay valid forever.
50	- **Sessions are per-tab, resumable, never auto-expire.** A session lives until the user detaches. Multiple agents can attach to the same run — second one in is read-only; takeover is an explicit verb.
51	- **Defaults exist but never auto-attach.** Per-user default project, per-project default timeline. Both feed the suggestion in `status`. Every new tab makes an explicit choice, every time, even if there's only one option.
52	- **Plans are mutable, but append-only once a step is dispatched.** Steps can be added, edited, or removed by the agent or human. **Undispatched steps** can be removed (tombstone-and-skip) or edited freely. **Dispatched steps are immutable** — "editing" a dispatched step means writing a new step *version* that supersedes it for any not-yet-dispatched work; the original stays in place for audit. Each mutation is a hash-chained event in `events.jsonl`. `plan.json` is the *initial* plan; the *effective* plan is `plan.json` + replayed mutations. The cursor stores `(step_id, step_version, dispatch_event_hash)` — not an index into a replayed plan — so a mutation can never silently move the cursor onto different work.
53	- **Run lease epoch fences all writes — inside a real critical section.** Every run carries a `writer_epoch: int` in `runs/<ulid>/lease.json`. The fence is only valid if **epoch check + last-hash compare + event append happen atomically**. The append path takes a `flock` on `events.jsonl`, re-reads the tail to confirm the previous-hash matches what the writer started from, re-reads the lease epoch, then writes — all under the same lock. A stale writer that passes its initial epoch check but loses the race to a takeover gets rejected at append time, not silently committed. Takeover atomically increments the epoch in the same locked write that swaps `attached_session.json`. The takeover *event* is observability; the lock + CAS is the actual fence.
54	- **One step type at runtime.** `requires_ack: bool`, `assignee`, `produces`, `repeat`, optional `command` (leaf) or `children` (group). Collapses `code` / `attested` / `nested` and the `AckRule` `agent` / `actor` split.
55	- **At authoring time, two template kinds.** Leaf-templates (the reusable building blocks — what we used to call executors) and **plan-templates** (reusable composition units — the role the orchestrator concept used to fill). Runtime is uniform; authoring keeps the composition primitive so hype-style work doesn't degenerate into ad-hoc plan-mutation soup. The pack layout on disk stays — only the conceptual frame changes.
56	- **Execution is location-agnostic via three explicit adapters: `local`, `manual`, `remote-artifact`.** Each step declares its adapter; the framework never assumes local execution. The schema is uniform; the adapters are not. **`local` and `manual` ship in Sprint 3; `remote-artifact` ships in Sprint 5a alongside the hype port** — hype's RunPod renders are the actual first client, so building the adapter against a real consumer keeps it honest.
57	  - `local` — subprocess runs in the project root; outputs land directly in `produces/`.
58	  - `manual` — agent (or human) runs the work somewhere out-of-band, then either `astrid ack` (sync) or writes a completion file to inbox (async). The agent is responsible for getting outputs into `produces/`.
59	  - `remote-artifact` — framework knows about a remote run (RunPod, ssh job, etc.). The adapter owns dispatch / status polling / artifact fetch / checksum / failure semantics. Completion is "remote done AND artifacts pulled AND checksums match." Half-completed remote work (job succeeded, fetch failed) is an explicit retry-able state, not a silent loss. This adapter is its own small job system; treating it as equal-weight with `local`/`manual` understates its complexity.
60	- **Async is the default, not a special case.** Any step can be slow. The agent dispatches, optionally closes the tab, returns later, calls `astrid next` which consumes inbox and advances.
61	- **Anything that produces an audit-worthy artifact must be a step.** Including ad-hoc python, scripts written on the fly, one-off tool runs. Friction is `astrid plan add-step --command '...' --produces '...'` + `astrid next`. Truly ephemeral commands (ls, grep, reading a file) don't need it.
62	- **Final outputs are first-class and integrity-checked.** Timeline manifest has `final_outputs: [{ulid, path, kind, size, sha256, check_status, check_at, recorded_at, recorded_by, from_run}]`. The path is a pointer to wherever the file actually lives; `size` and `sha256` are captured at finalize time so the audit record stands even if the file later moves or rots. `check_status` is `ok` / `missing` / `mismatch` (recomputed on `astrid timeline show` if you ask). `kind` is free text — mp4, transcript, training-set, process-doc, anything. Multiple allowed per timeline.
63	- **Cost is a first-class but optional field on a step.** Code that knows the cost (LLM SDK response, RunPod billing) declares it on completion. No automatic tracking. Aggregable up to run, timeline, project.
64	- **Detailed assignee taxonomy:** `system | agent:<id> | any-agent | human:<name> | any-human`. Set per-step in the plan, never inferred from session identity. Explicit `astrid claim` verb; agent can claim for itself or on behalf of a human.
65	- **Stable agent identity in `~/.astrid/identity.json`.** Carries `agent_id: <slug>` (e.g. `claude-1`, `codex-research`) — the canonical id used in `agent:<id>` assignees and ack `--agent` flags. Sessions inherit it; a session may override per-tab via `astrid attach --as agent:<id>` for occasional multi-agent setups. Without an identity file, `astrid` errors on first run and prompts the user to create one. Single-user doesn't remove the need for stable agent ids.
66	- **Migrate everything, no shims.** Single user; no compat burden. One-shot rewrites for `active_run.json`, threads, per-project/per-run `timeline.json`, existing `plan.json` files.
67	- **Project slugs are unique under the same root.** `astrid projects create` blocks duplicates. Across different roots is fine.
68	- **`astrid status` is the agent's mandated anchor.** SKILL.md's first instruction is "run status before any other verb; if unbound, ask the user which project and timeline." The stop-hook re-injects this when context decays. CLI gates every other verb behind a session binding.
69	
70	### State machines (authoritative)
71	
72	These are the only valid transitions. Any verb or adapter that wants to push state through one of these objects must emit the corresponding event; any state not reachable by these arrows is a bug.
73	
74	**Run:**
75	```
76	created → in-flight → completed
77	                    → aborted
78	in-flight ↔ in-flight   (plan_mutated, claim, unclaim, takeover bumps writer_epoch)
79	```
80	
81	**Step:**
82	```
83	declared → dispatched → completed
84	                      → awaiting-fetch → completed       (remote-artifact)
85	                                       → failed-fetch    (terminal until retry-fetch)
86	                      → ack-pending   → completed        (requires_ack: ack approve)
87	                                      → retrying         (ack retry → re-dispatch)
88	                                      → failed           (ack abort scopes to step)
89	declared → tombstoned                                    (undispatched only)
90	dispatched → superseded-by-vN                            (immutable; new version takes over for unstarted iterations/items only)
91	```
92	
93	**Iteration / item (inside a repeat step):**
94	```
95	pending → started → completed
96	                  → failed → started     (next iteration / item attempt)
97	                           → exhausted   (terminal; pins the step as failed)
98	```
99	
100	Anything not in these tables — "edited mid-flight", "rewound to before dispatch", "split across two writers" — is rejected by the kernel.
101	
102	### How this gets executed
103	
104	The reshape is itself executed via Astrid's task system (megaplan). Per-sprint planning, decision logging, sprint-entry context loading, exit rituals, risk tracking, and acceptance gating all live in megaplan as runs and steps. This doc captures *what* to build and *in what order* — the engineering substance. It deliberately does not specify a parallel paperwork track. The only process-shaped artifacts inside individual sprints are **stop-lines** (engineering halt-conditions that override "push through"), which are listed in each sprint's Decisions block.
105	
106	### Explicitly out of scope for V1
107	
108	- Fan-out / fan-in across sibling steps — sequential cursor only. `depends_on` is a reserved field for later.
109	- Automatic cost tracking — only what code declares.
110	- Multi-user identity proof — `--actor bob` is taken at face value.
111	- CAS layer (the Phase 7 stub stays a stub).
112	- Bundle import — export exists, round-trip is later.
113	- Orchestrator versioning / project-pinning to a specific orchestrator version.
114	
115	---
116	
117	## Sprint 0 — Prerequisites (~3 days)
118	
119	**Goal:** stand up the scaffolding the reshape sprints assume but don't own. Nothing here ships user-visible behavior; everything here makes the next eight weeks survivable.
120	
121	### Decisions
122	
123	- **Long-lived `reshape/` branch off main.** One feature branch per sprint, merged into `reshape/`. `reshape/` only merges to main after Sprint 5a soak. Half-migrated state on disk + half-shipped code on main is the worst case; this branch strategy avoids it.
124	- **Snapshot before everything.** `astrid-projects/` tarball stored outside the repo, dated. Re-snapshot at every sprint entry. This is the data-rollback for every migration.
125	- **Pinned regression workload.** Pick one specific past hype run (transcript + brief + final mp4). Re-run it end-to-end as the regression gate at every sprint exit. If a sprint breaks it, the sprint isn't shipped.
126	- **Two spikes are pre-Sprint-1, not in Sprint 1.** Env inheritance and macOS APFS flock semantics. Both are ~20–60 lines of test code; both are load-bearing for Sprint 1's design. If either spike's result invalidates an assumption, Sprint 1 redesigns *before* coding rather than mid-sprint.
127	
128	### Deliverables
129

1	"""Crockford ULID helpers for thread, run, and group records."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import os
6	import re
7	import threading
8	import time
9	
10	CROCKFORD_ALPHABET = "0123456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTVWXYZ"
11	ULID_LENGTH = 26
12	ULID_RE = re.compile(r"^[0123456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTVWXYZ]{26}$")
13	
14	_RANDOM_BITS = 80
15	_RANDOM_MASK = (1 << _RANDOM_BITS) - 1
16	_STATE_LOCK = threading.Lock()
17	_LAST_MS = -1
18	_LAST_RANDOM = 0
19	
20	
21	def generate_ulid() -> str:
22	    """Return a 26-character Crockford ULID, monotonic within this process."""
23	    global _LAST_MS, _LAST_RANDOM
24	
25	    now_ms = int(time.time() * 1000)
26	    with _STATE_LOCK:
27	        if now_ms > _LAST_MS:
28	            _LAST_MS = now_ms
29	            _LAST_RANDOM = int.from_bytes(os.urandom(10), "big")
30	        else:
31	            _LAST_RANDOM = (_LAST_RANDOM + 1) & _RANDOM_MASK
32	            if _LAST_RANDOM == 0:
33	                while now_ms <= _LAST_MS:
34	                    time.sleep(0.001)
35	                    now_ms = int(time.time() * 1000)
36	                _LAST_MS = now_ms
37	                _LAST_RANDOM = int.from_bytes(os.urandom(10), "big")
38	        value = (_LAST_MS << _RANDOM_BITS) | _LAST_RANDOM
39	    return _encode_crockford(value)
40	
41	
42	def generate_thread_id() -> str:
43	    return generate_ulid()
44	
45	
46	def generate_run_id() -> str:
47	    return generate_ulid()
48	
49	
50	def generate_group_id() -> str:
51	    return generate_ulid()
52	
53	
54	def is_ulid(value: object) -> bool:
55	    return isinstance(value, str) and ULID_RE.fullmatch(value) is not None
56	
57	
58	def require_ulid(value: object, field: str = "id") -> str:
59	    if not is_ulid(value):
60	        raise ValueError(f"{field} must be a 26-character Crockford ULID")
61	    return str(value)
62	
63	
64	def _encode_crockford(value: int) -> str:
65	    chars = ["0"] * ULID_LENGTH
66	    for index in range(ULID_LENGTH - 1, -1, -1):
67	        chars[index] = CROCKFORD_ALPHABET[value & 0x1F]
68	        value >>= 5
69	    return "".join(chars)
70

Critique complete. Top concerns:

1. **FLAG-001 / scope**: `astrid/threads/` removal is far larger than the plan accounts for — 10+ production modules (`core/orchestrator/runner.py`, `core/executor/runner.py`, every pack `run.py`) wrap their work through `thread_wrapper.*`. Plan hides this in one bullet.
2. **FLAG-002 / binding model**: Removing `active_run.json` without any project-level pointer leaves `cmd_attach <project>` and the migration script unable to pick "the current run" when a project has multiple historical runs.
3. **FLAG-003 / invariant regression**: Current code enforces "one active run per project" at `cmd_start`. Plan silently drops that without saying whether multiple in-flight runs are now allowed.
4. **FLAG-008 / hype regression**: Phase 5 deletes threads before any runner rewrite — but the hype regression workload runs through `orchestrator/runner.py` which uses `thread_wrapper`. Acceptance criteria can't be met under current ordering.
5. **FLAG-009 / test-suite churn**: 18 existing tests directly import `active_run` helpers or set up `active_run.json` fixtures; `[pass_to_pass]` tier requires them to keep passing.

Six additional flags cover ULID duplication (existing one in threads/ids.py has stronger monotonicity guarantees), CLI-gate enforcement boundary, inbox re-entry into `gate_command`, wrapper retry semantics, and ambiguous criteria. Findings filed across all 8 checks.

        ### Task Summary
        Sprint 1 of the Astrid reshape introduces per-tab sessions, a `runs/<ulid>/lease.json` writer-epoch, and — most critically — a flock-protected event-append helper with last-hash + epoch CAS that replaces the current unsafe `append_event` in `astrid/core/task/events.py`. All mutating verbs and inbox consumption must route through that locked path. Sprint 1 also adds `~/.astrid` identity/config/sessions plumbing, a new `astrid attach / sessions / status` CLI surface with a session gate on every other verb, deletes `<project>/active_run.json` and the `astrid threads` subsystem (migrating both into session bindings), and writes a `default_timeline_id: null` sentinel into `project.json` for Sprint 2.

        ### Constraints
        - Sprint 1 must NOT change the step model. No edits to plan.py / inbox.py (beyond switching to the locked append helper and dropping clear_active_run) / step adapter logic.
- Sprint 1 must NOT implement the timeline container — only write a `default_timeline_id: null` sentinel into project.json.
- Sprint 1 must NOT touch RunPod / vibecomfy / hype port / auditing verbs (`run show`, `artifacts`, `trace`) — those are Sprints 5a / 5b.
- The locked-append helper must hold ONE flock(LOCK_EX) across last-hash re-read + epoch re-read + append + fsync. Splitting these is the bug.
- Both CAS failures (stale tail, stale epoch) must raise typed exceptions including the conflicting values in the message — no silent fallbacks anywhere in the lease/append path.
- Takeover must atomically increment writer_epoch in the SAME locked write that swaps attached_session.json. The takeover event is observability; the lock + CAS is the fence.
- All existing Sprint 0 tests must continue to pass (env-inheritance spike, flock-APFS spike, two-tab harness smoke).
- Migration scripts default to --dry-run; --apply commits; idempotent; log per-action to stderr; per-project audit log to temp file. Any data loss in `active_run.json` migration is a STOP-LINE.
- Migration script that adds `default_timeline_id` must keep `project.json` valid against the existing validator (decide additive vs schema-version-bump).
- Astrid is now a SESSION-FIRST CLI: only attach, status, projects ls, projects create, sessions ls, sessions takeover, init, --help are allowed unbound. Every other verb errors with a clear hint.
- Subprocesses must inherit ASTRID_SESSION_ID — confirmed by Sprint 0 env-inheritance spike; do not silently scrub env in any new code path.
- SKILL.md and AGENTS.md are symlinks to astrid/packs/_core/skill/SKILL.md — edit the target. Stop-hook preamble in lifecycle.py:_AGENT_MD_TEMPLATE + PROHIBITION_PREAMBLE must be updated to re-inject the new status-first instruction.
- Branch must be `reshape/sprint-1` off `reshape/sprint-0`.
- Type hints everywhere; Pathlib over os.path; no comments unless the WHY is non-obvious.

        ### Suggested Approach
        Build bottom-up so each layer is independently testable before integrating into the CLI:

1. **Branch + scaffold.** `git checkout -b reshape/sprint-1 reshape/sprint-0`. Create `astrid/core/session/` package with empty modules: paths.py, model.py, binding.py, lease.py, identity.py, config.py, discovery.py.

2. **Lease + locked-append (the apex).** First, define `class StaleTailError(EventLogError)` and `class StaleEpochError(EventLogError)` in events.py. Then write `append_event_locked(run_dir: Path, event: dict, *, expected_writer_epoch: int, expected_prev_hash: str) -> dict` that:
   - opens `run_dir / 'events.jsonl'` in append mode
   - `fcntl.flock(fh.fileno(), fcntl.LOCK_EX)` (blocking)
   - reads the current tail line; parses its hash; if `last_hash != expected_prev_hash` raise StaleTailError including both
   - reads `run_dir / 'lease.json'`'s writer_epoch; if `current != expected_writer_epoch` raise StaleEpochError
   - computes new hash from `last_hash`, writes the JSON line + newline, flushes, `os.fsync`, releases lock
   - returns the stored dict
   Keep the existing `append_event` as a thin wrapper that reads epoch + prev_hash internally — that lets legacy single-writer paths keep working without the CAS, but they still get serialized by flock so they never interleave.
   Unit-test in `tests/task/test_events_locked.py` (happy path, stale tail, stale epoch, succeeds after epoch bump).
   Then run the flock-spike pattern but invoke the new helper from two subprocesses and assert exactly-one-winner.

3. **Session model + paths.** In `session/model.py` define `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Session(id, project, timeline, run_id, agent_id, attached_at, last_used_at, role)` with `from_path/to_path` JSON helpers. In `session/paths.py` mirror `core/project/paths.py` patterns: env-var override `ASTRID_HOME` (default `~/.astrid`), helpers for `sessions_dir()`, `identity_path()`, `user_config_path()`, `workspace_config_path()`. ULID: vendor a ~20-line generator (or pull `python-ulid`) — record the choice in a one-line comment. Round-trip tests for all readers/writers.

4. **Lease.** `session/lease.py`: `read_lease(run_dir)`, `write_lease_init(run_dir, session_id, writer_epoch=0)`, `bump_epoch_and_swap_session(run_dir, new_session_id) -> int` which acquires the SAME flock on events.jsonl, increments writer_epoch, swaps attached_session.json, appends a takeover event, releases. Reuse `append_event_locked` so the takeover event itself is properly chained.

5. **Binding.** `session/binding.py::resolve_current() -> Session | None` reads `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` env, looks up the session file. `is_writer_for(session, run_dir) -> bool` compares lease.attached_session_id.

6. **Identity + config + discovery.** Identity reader/writer + first-run bootstrap (prompts for agent_id slug). Config reader merges `~/.astrid/config.json` + `.astrid/config.json` (workspace wins). Discovery enumerates projects under `resolve_projects_root()`.

7. **Verb implementations.** Add a new module `astrid/core/session/cli.py` with `cmd_attach`, `cmd_sessions_ls`, `cmd_sessions_detach`, `cmd_sessions_takeover`. Rewrite `cmd_status` in lifecycle.py (or move it) to be session-driven and print the full breadcrumb. cmd_status with no identity → bootstrap; with identity but no session → list projects + attach hint; bound → breadcrumb.

8. **CLI gate.** In `astrid/pipeline.py:main`, after the existing nudge and before `_dispatch`, add a gate: if first token is NOT in the allowlist `{attach, sessions, status, projects, init, -h, --help}` (and projects' sub-verb must be ls/create when unbound), require ASTRID_SESSION_ID to resolve a valid session, else print the error + recovery. Wire `attach`, `sessions` dispatch entries. Remove the `thread` dispatch entry.

9. **Migrate callers off active_run.py.** Sweep the 4+2 sites in lifecycle.py, gate.py:519, inbox.py:216, hook.py:40/66, orchestrate/test_runner.py:113. Replace with: resolve session → load run_dir → use lease for CAS. Route every `append_event(events_path, ev)` call through `append_event_locked(run_dir, ev, expected_writer_epoch=epoch, expected_prev_hash=prev)` where `epoch` is read at the start of the verb and `prev_hash` is read just before append. On StaleEpochError/StaleTailError, the verb exits non-zero with a clean message — the agent retries.

10. **Threads removal.** Delete `astrid/threads/`, the pipeline.py:123-126 dispatch entry, the 13 test_threads_*.py files. Update SKILL.md (the symlink target) to remove the line-60 instruction and replace with the new attach/status workflow. Run the SKILL inspect test to see what assumes threads — likely test_threads_docs_skill_inspect.py is deleted along with the rest.

11. **project.json sentinel.** Add `default_timeline_id` as optional in `build_project`. Update `validate_project` to preserve it (it already preserves unknown fields, but make it explicit + typed).

12. **Migration scripts under `scripts/migrations/sprint-1/`:**
    - `migrate_active_run_to_sessions.py` — for each `<project>/active_run.json`, materialize one session binding (using the project root's name as slug, ULID-generated session id) and delete the file.
    - `migrate_threads_to_sessions.py` — read `.astrid/threads.json` (and any per-project thread state), fold any persistent fields (e.g., variant selections) into session bindings or, if irrelevant after threads die, log + skip. Confirm with the user that no run-history field is being dropped.
    - `migrate_set_default_timeline_sentinel.py` — for each `project.json`, ensure `default_timeline_id` key exists with `null`.
    All three: `#!/usr/bin/env python3`, executable, `--dry-run` default, `--apply` flag, stderr per-action log, temp-file audit log per project.

13. **SKILL.md / AGENTS.md rewrite.** Step 1: `astrid status` always. Document `attach`, `next`, `ack`, `sessions ls/detach/takeover`. Update PROHIBITION_PREAMBLE and `_AGENT_MD_TEMPLATE` in lifecycle.py so the stop-hook re-injection teaches the new model.

14. **Tests + harness validation.** Add the listed test files. Run the two-tab harness 100 rounds on a real mutating verb (pick `astrid ack <step> --decision approve` because it has the cleanest single-event-append surface, or even a thin `astrid sessions takeover` test that exercises lease swap). Confirm exactly-one-winner. Then run the takeover-during-append test under multiple seeds.

15. **Regression sweep.** Run all of `pytest tests/` (excluding deleted thread tests). Fix any failure that turns out to be a real escape rather than test rot. Confirm Sprint 0 spike + harness tests still green. Run any hype-related smoke test under the new model and confirm functional non-regression (no byte-equivalence required per regression-workload.md).

16. **Stop-line checks before merge.**
    - Two-tab harness: 100 rounds, exactly one winner per round, no interleaving in events.jsonl.
    - Tab restart + ASTRID_SESSION_ID re-export restores binding.
    - active_run migration: dry-run shows expected actions; --apply produces session bindings + deletes file; events.jsonl unchanged; no run-history loss.

        The engineering brief above was produced by analyzing the codebase. Use it as a strong starting point, but the suggested approach is a hypothesis — verify it's the best fix, not just a valid one.

        Idea:
# Sprint 1 — Sessions and the binding contract

This is Sprint 1 of the Astrid reshape (full plan: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid/idea.md` — Sprint 1 section is canonical for scope; this brief expands it for execution).

Working directory: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid`. Branch: create `reshape/sprint-1` off `reshape/sprint-0` and work there.

## Goal

End multi-tab races. Make "what am I bound to" a first-class, queryable thing. Ship `astrid status` as the agent's mandated anchor verb. Lay down the lease + locked-append contract every later sprint depends on.

**No timelines yet (Sprint 2). No new step model yet (Sprint 3).** Sprint 1 is purely the session/binding/safety layer.

## Foundational invariant (the apex contract)

The single most important deliverable is the **locked event-append path**, because every later sprint runs on top of it. The contract is:

> Every event appended to `runs/<ulid>/events.jsonl` does this **atomically inside one critical section** held by `flock(events.jsonl, LOCK_EX)`:
> 1. Re-read the file's last line, parse its hash, compare against the writer's read-time hash. If they differ → reject with a stale-tail error.
> 2. Re-read `runs/<ulid>/lease.json`'s `writer_epoch`. Compare against the writer's read-time epoch. If they differ → reject with a stale-epoch error.
> 3. Compute the new event's previous-hash = the last-line hash.
> 4. Append the new event line.
> 5. fsync (or equivalent durability), then release the lock.

A stale writer that passed its initial epoch check but lost the race to a takeover **gets rejected at append time, not silently committed**. The `flock` + last-hash CAS + epoch CAS are the actual fence; the takeover *event* is just observability.

This replaces the current `append_event` in `astrid/core/task/events.py`, which does read/verify/append **without a file lock** (the bug we're fixing).

The Sprint 0 flock-on-APFS spike confirmed `flock` is reliable for this workload. Use the worked test pattern from `tests/spikes/test_flock_apfs.py` as the integration-test starting point.

## Decisions (locked; do not relitigate)

- **Session id = ULID.** Stored in `~/.astrid/sessions/<ulid>.json` with `{session_id, project, timeline, run_id, agent_id, attached_at, last_used_at, role}`. `role` ∈ `writer | reader`.
- **`ASTRID_SESSION_ID` env var binds the tab.** Subprocesses inherit through `fork`/`exec`. Sprint 0 verified all existing subprocess paths preserve env via `{**os.environ, ...}`.
- **Stable agent identity in `~/.astrid/identity.json`** with `agent_id: <slug>` (e.g. `claude-1`, `codex-research`). First-run bootstrap prompts user to set one. Sessions inherit it; per-tab override via `astrid attach --as agent:<id>`.
- **Per-user default project in `~/.astrid/config.json`; per-workspace override in `.astrid/config.json`.** Neither auto-attaches — both feed the suggestion shown by `status` when unbound.
- **Per-project default timeline pointer recorded in `project.json`** (Sprint 1 writes the field; Sprint 2 wires it into `astrid attach`). Use a sentinel value in S1's migration; Sprint 2 backfills.
- **Read-only attach when a run is held by another session.** Second attacher gets `role: reader`. Takeover is the explicit verb `astrid sessions takeover <session_id>`.
- **Run lease epoch (`writer_epoch: int`).** Stored in `runs/<ulid>/lease.json` alongside `attached_session.json` (the current writer's session id). Every mutating verb routes through the locked-append helper above.
- **Takeover atomically increments `writer_epoch`** in the same locked write that swaps `attached_session.json`. Both old session and new session see a `takeover` event in `events.jsonl` with `{prev_session, new_session, prev_epoch, new_epoch, at}`.
- **Stuck-attachment recovery via `status`.** A fresh tab's `status` proactively flags suspected-dead sessions (criterion: no event written in N seconds AND session-file mtime older than M seconds — pick honest defaults like 60s/300s) and offers takeover with a "may still be live elsewhere — confirm" warning. Manual `astrid sessions detach <id>` is the unconditional escape hatch.
- **Threads die.** `thread show @active` and friends are removed. Any persistent thread state migrates into session bindings in a one-shot pass.
- **Inbox primitive standardized.** Every run has `runs/<ulid>/inbox/`. Sessions and external systems write JSON files; `astrid next` consumes them. This is the substrate that makes async work possible from day one.
- **First-run bootstrap is part of this sprint, not assumed.** Fresh tab post-migration must not produce a string of errors. When `~/.astrid/identity.json` is missing OR no default project is set, `astrid` prompts through identity + project discovery + default selection explicitly. `astrid status` when unbound + no default lists discoverable projects with the `attach` command spelled out (not just "no session").
- **Migration sets a per-project default-timeline sentinel** even though Sprint 2 wires the actual timeline container. Otherwise Sprint 2 has to backfill across the entire project tree.
- **CLI gate.** Every verb except `attach`, `status`, `projects ls`, `projects create`, `sessions ls`, `sessions takeover`, `init` (first-run bootstrap), and `--help` errors out with a clear "no session bound" message that suggests `astrid attach <project>`.

## Deliverables

### New / rewritten files

- **`astrid/core/session/`** new package:
  - `paths.py` — helpers for `~/.astrid/sessions/<ulid>.json`, `~/.astrid/identity.json`, `~/.astrid/config.json`, `.astrid/config.json` (workspace).
  - `model.py` — `Session` dataclass (id, project, timeline, run_id, agent_id, attached_at, last_used_at, role) with json read/write.
  - `binding.py` — resolve current session via `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` env var; readers and writers; "is this session a writer for this run" checks.
  - `lease.py` — `runs/<ulid>/lease.json` reader/writer; `attached_session.json` reader/writer.
  - `identity.py` — `~/.astrid/identity.json` reader/writer + first-run bootstrap.
  - `config.py` — config file reader (user + workspace) + default project/timeline resolution.
  - `discovery.py` — list discoverable projects under the projects root for the bootstrap UX.

- **`astrid/core/task/events.py`** rewrite the append path:
  - New `append_event_locked(run_dir, event_dict, *, expected_writer_epoch, expected_prev_hash) -> EventRecord` that does flock + last-hash CAS + epoch CAS atomically. Raises `StaleTailError` / `StaleEpochError` on conflict.
  - Old `append_event` becomes a thin wrapper over the locked path that reads epoch + last-hash internally; deprecate it for callers that need the CAS guarantees.
  - All existing call sites that mutate the event log MUST route through the new locked helper.

- **`astrid/core/task/active_run.py`** delete or stub:
  - The `<project>/active_run.json` file is the multi-tab race we're fixing. Migrate any existing files into per-tab session bindings (one-shot at `astrid attach`), then remove the read/write helpers. Leave a one-line stub explaining what replaced it.

- **Threads subsystem removal:**
  - Find every `thread show @active`-style verb and any `astrid/core/thread/` (or wherever threads live). Migrate persistent thread state into session bindings (best-effort one-shot migration script). Then remove the subsystem.

### New / rewritten verbs

Use the existing CLI module (`astrid/cli/` or wherever verbs are registered — discover by reading current code).

- `astrid attach <project> [--timeline <slug>] [--session <id>] [--as agent:<id>]` — bind the current tab to a project. Creates a new session (writes `~/.astrid/sessions/<ulid>.json`), prints the `ASTRID_SESSION_ID=<ulid>` line for the user to `export`. If `--session <id>` given, resume an existing session. If a run is held by another writer, attach as reader and tell the user how to take over.
- `astrid sessions ls` — list all sessions in `~/.astrid/sessions/` with their bound project + timeline + run + last-used.
- `astrid sessions detach [<id>]` — explicit detach. With no id, detaches the current tab.
- `astrid sessions takeover <run-id|session-id>` — atomic epoch-bump + lease swap. Emits a `takeover` event into the run's `events.jsonl`. Refuses if the session being taken over wrote within the last N seconds without confirmation flag.
- `astrid status` — completely rewritten. When bound, prints the full breadcrumb: `session <id> · agent <id> · project <slug> · timeline <slug> · run <id> · current step <name> · recent events (last 5) · inbox count · role (writer/reader) · takeover hint when reader`. When unbound: lists discoverable projects, suggests defaults if set, spells out the exact `astrid attach <slug>` command. When `~/.astrid/identity.json` missing: triggers the first-run bootstrap.
- **CLI gate** on every other verb: read `ASTRID_SESSION_ID`, look up the session file, error-out with a clear hint if either is missing.

### Migration scripts

Place under `scripts/migrations/sprint-1/`:

- `migrate_active_run_to_sessions.py` — for each project with an `active_run.json`, materialize a session binding for "the calling tab" (best-effort: one session per project), then delete the file. Idempotent. Dry-run by default; `--apply` flag to commit.
- `migrate_threads_to_sessions.py` — same shape for thread state, if it exists in the codebase.
- `migrate_set_default_timeline_sentinel.py` — for each project, write a sentinel `default_timeline_id: null` field into `project.json` so Sprint 2 doesn't have to backfill.

Each script:
- Logs every action to stderr.
- Supports `--dry-run` (default) and `--apply`.
- Writes a per-project audit log to a temp file.

### SKILL.md / AGENTS.md rewrite

Both files are stale (predate the task framework, don't teach `astrid status` / `next` / `ack`). Rewrite around the new mandated workflow:

1. **First instruction**: "Run `astrid status` before any other verb. If unbound, ask the user which project."
2. Document `attach`, `status`, `next`, `ack`, `sessions ls/detach/takeover`.
3. Stop-hook preamble (if Astrid has one — discover by reading current SKILL.md) is updated to re-inject the status-first instruction when context decays.

### Tests

- **Locked-append unit tests** (`tests/task/test_events_locked.py`): single-process correctness — append, append-after-stale-tail rejected, append-after-stale-epoch rejected, append after epoch bump succeeds.
- **Two-tab integration tests** (use the existing `tests/concurrency/two_tab_harness.py` from Sprint 0): two writers race the same run; exactly one succeeds, the other gets stale-tail or stale-epoch. Run with `--count 100` to surface races.
- **Takeover atomicity test**: takeover-during-append must either (a) the append wins and takeover sees stale tail and retries, or (b) the takeover wins and the appender sees stale epoch and rejects. Never both succeed; never silent loss.
- **Session attach/detach/resume tests** (unit + small integration).
- **Migration script tests**: each script has a smoke test with a tiny fixture project tree.
- **First-run bootstrap test**: simulate missing `~/.astrid/identity.json` → bootstrap prompt path runs end-to-end (mock the prompt input).
- **CLI gate test**: every gated verb errors clearly when `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` is unset.

## Stop-lines (halt and rethink, do not push through)

1. **If the locked-append path can't be proven correct under contention** (the two-tab harness shows interleaved writes or silent epoch-violations under any seed), halt. The whole reshape is built on this guarantee.
2. **If session resume across tab restart is broken** (closing a tab + reopening + `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` re-export doesn't restore the binding), halt. The "sessions are resumable, never auto-expire" decision is load-bearing.
3. **If migration of `active_run.json` causes ANY data loss** (any project's run-history becomes unreadable), halt and restore from the Sprint 0 snapshot.

## Out of scope (Sprint 2 / 3 territory — do NOT touch)

- Timeline as container (Sprint 2). The `default_timeline_id` field is a sentinel only; do not implement the timeline schema.
- Step model rewrite (Sprint 3). `plan.py` / `inbox.py` / step adapter logic stays as-is; only the event-append path changes.
- Plan mutation verbs (Sprint 3).
- Any RunPod / vibecomfy work.
- Hype port.
- Auditing verbs (`astrid run show / artifacts / trace`) — those are Sprint 5a.

## Acceptance criteria

- `git branch --show-current` returns `reshape/sprint-1`.
- All Sprint 0 tests still pass (regression).
- `pytest tests/task/test_events_locked.py tests/concurrency/ tests/session/ -v` all green.
- Two-tab harness: race the same `astrid attach` + locked-append-via-some-verb 100 times; exactly one writer succeeds per race, the other gets a clean stale-tail or stale-epoch error.
- Fresh tab + no identity file → `astrid status` triggers first-run bootstrap, completes successfully, leaves `~/.astrid/identity.json` written.
- Fresh tab + identity but no session bound → `astrid status` lists discoverable projects + spells out `astrid attach <slug>`.
- Fresh tab + session bound → `astrid status` prints the full breadcrumb.
- `astrid sessions takeover` of a stale-looking session works; the old session's next mutating verb gets rejected with stale-epoch.
- All migration scripts run cleanly against the Sprint 0 baseline inventory (refer to `docs/reshape/inventory-baseline-20260511.csv`).
- The Sprint 0 pinned regression workload (`docs/reshape/regression-workload.md`) re-runs cleanly. **Note:** that doc reports no past hype runs exist on this machine, so the regression gate is "no functional regression" rather than "byte-equivalent output." Run any existing hype-related smoke tests and confirm green.
- SKILL.md / AGENTS.md updated; a fresh-context agent reading them can complete one full task end-to-end without asking a clarifying question.

## Useful repo references

- `astrid/core/task/events.py` — the unsafe `append_event` to replace.
- `astrid/core/task/active_run.py` — the file to migrate away from.
- `astrid/core/project/paths.py` — project / run path helpers; reuse the patterns.
- `tests/spikes/test_flock_apfs.py` — Sprint 0's flock spike; the worked pattern for the locked-append integration tests.
- `tests/concurrency/two_tab_harness.py` — Sprint 0's race harness.
- `docs/reshape/spike-env-inheritance.md` — confirms `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` survives every subprocess path.
- `docs/reshape/spike-flock-apfs.md` — confirms `flock` is reliable for this workload.
- `idea.md` § Foundation > Load-bearing decisions — apex spec.
- `idea.md` § Sprint 1 — the canonical sprint definition this brief expands.

## Style notes

- Type hints everywhere. Pathlib over os.path.
- New tests follow existing test naming + structure conventions in the repo.
- Migration scripts: shebang, `chmod +x`, `--dry-run` is the default.
- No silent fallbacks in the lease/append path — every CAS failure raises a typed exception with the conflicting values in the message.

# Implementation Plan: Astrid Sprint 1 — Sessions & the Binding Contract (v2)

## Overview

Sprint 1 of the Astrid reshape on branch `reshape/sprint-1` off `reshape/sprint-0`. The apex deliverable is a **flock-protected, last-hash + writer-epoch CAS event-append helper** that replaces the unsafe `append_event` at `astrid/core/task/events.py:25`. Around it: per-tab ULID sessions in `~/.astrid/sessions/`, a run-side `lease.json` with `writer_epoch`, a project-level `current_run.json` pointer (the renamed-and-stripped successor to `active_run.json`), an `astrid attach / sessions / status` CLI surface gated on every other verb, SKILL.md rewrite to mandate `astrid status` as step 1, and a `default_timeline_id: null` sentinel into `project.json`.

**Scope contraction from v1 (in response to flags FLAG-001, FLAG-008, scope-1, scope-3, issue_hints-2):** `astrid/threads/` is imported by 18 production sites (`orchestrator/runner.py:25`, `executor/runner.py:25`, `orchestrator/cli.py:272`, `executor/cli.py:439`, `project/run.py:12`, `packs/builtin/{generate_image,logo_ideas,iteration_video}/run.py`, `packs/iteration/{prepare,assemble}/run.py`, plus `pipeline.py:124`). Fully ripping it out is multi-sprint surgery and would break hype regression (which routes through `orchestrator/runner.py` `thread_wrapper.begin_orchestrator_run` and `subprocess_env`). **Sprint 1 removes only the user-facing `astrid thread` CLI verb + its dispatch + the SKILL.md instruction; `astrid/threads/` stays as an internal library that runners/packs continue to use.** The 13 `tests/test_threads_*.py` files stay (they test internal library code that still exists); only `test_threads_docs_skill_inspect.py` is updated to match the new SKILL.md text. Variant-selection migration becomes a no-op (data is untouched). A later sprint retires `astrid/threads/` as part of the runner rewrite.

**Active-run replacement (FLAG-002, FLAG-003, correctness-1, correctness-2):** delete the racy `<project>/active_run.json` semantics (writer fence on a project-level file) but keep a **project-level current-run pointer** at `<project>/current_run.json` with the minimal contract `{"run_id": "<ulid>"}` (no plan_hash; plan_hash CAS moves to the run-side lease). Written by `astrid start`; cleared by `astrid abort` and on natural completion. **Preserves the "one active run per project" invariant** via existing cmd_start guard. The writer fence is `runs/<run_id>/lease.json` (`writer_epoch` + `attached_session_id`), enforced by `append_event_locked`.

**Confirmed touch-points:**
- 20 `append_event(` call sites (gate.py × 13 incl. `_gate_append` at 551; lifecycle.py × 2; inbox.py × 2; lifecycle_ack.py × 2).
- 7 `read_active_run` sites + 1 write + 2 clears.
- 18 test files use active_run fixtures directly — all enumerated in Step 11.
- Existing ULID at `astrid/threads/ids.py:generate_ulid()` (Crockford 26-char, monotonic via `_LAST_MS`/`_LAST_RANDOM`) is reused, not re-implemented (correctness-4).
- `consume_inbox_entry` at `inbox.py:259` calls `gate_command(slug, synthesized, [], root=projects_root)` — gate_command must thread the outer epoch through (callers-2).
- Stop-hook `hook.py:_walk_cwd_ancestors` (lines 38–52) + `_scan_projects_root` (lines 55–68); session-bound takes precedence (all_locations-1).
- `orchestrate/test_runner.py` is invoked from `astrid author test` (orchestrate/cli.py:50) — developer/CI path; allowlisted via explicit `--project` arg (callers-3).
- Docs to update: `astrid/packs/_core/skill/SKILL.md`, `docs/HOOKS.md`, `docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md` (all_locations-3).

## Phase 0: Branch & Scaffold

### Step 1: Branch and scaffold session package (`astrid/core/session/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Branch.** `git checkout -b reshape/sprint-1 reshape/sprint-0`.
2. **Scaffold** `astrid/core/session/` with empty modules + `__init__.py`: `paths.py`, `model.py`, `binding.py`, `lease.py`, `identity.py`, `config.py`, `discovery.py`, `cli.py`, `writer.py`, `constants.py`.
3. **Create** `scripts/migrations/sprint-1/` directory.

## Phase 1: The Apex — Locked Event Append

### Step 2: Typed CAS exceptions + locked helper (`astrid/core/task/events.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `StaleTailError(EventLogError)` and `StaleEpochError(EventLogError)` with `expected` and `actual` attributes; conflicting values in the message.
2. **Implement** `append_event_locked(run_dir: Path, event: dict[str, Any], *, expected_writer_epoch: int, expected_prev_hash: str) -> dict[str, Any]`:
   - Resolve `events_path = run_dir / "events.jsonl"`, `lease_path = run_dir / "lease.json"`. `events_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`.
   - Open events_path in `"a+b"` mode. `fcntl.flock(fh.fileno(), fcntl.LOCK_EX)` (blocking).
   - **Tail-seek read (NOT whole-file scan)** (correctness-5): `fh.seek(0, os.SEEK_END); size = fh.tell()`. Read last `min(size, 4096)` bytes; find the last `\n`; parse the trailing line. If empty file → `tail_hash = ZERO_HASH`. If the 4 KB window has no newline (single oversized line), expand to 64 KB then fail loudly. CAS against `expected_prev_hash`.
   - **Still inside lock**: read `lease.json` (using `Path.read_text`); extract `writer_epoch`. CAS against `expected_writer_epoch`.
   - Compute new hash via existing `_event_hash`. Write at end. `fh.flush()`, `os.fsync(fh.fileno())`. fsync parent on first-create. Release lock on close.
3. **`append_event(path, event)` becomes test/migration-only** (correctness-3): rewrite as a no-CAS-loop wrapper that reads epoch+tail from disk and calls `append_event_locked` once. **Document** at the top of the function: "Production code MUST use `WriterContext` from `astrid.core.session.writer`. This wrapper raises `StaleTailError`/`StaleEpochError` on contention and has no retry." Drop `verify_chain` from this path (kept as audit-only).
4. **Lock contract**: lock is `events.jsonl` itself (single fd serializes all writers, including takeover's lease swap which calls `append_event_locked` for the takeover event).

### Step 3: Unit tests (`tests/task/test_events_locked.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `test_append_event_locked_happy_path`, `test_append_event_rejects_stale_tail`, `test_append_event_rejects_stale_epoch`, `test_append_event_succeeds_after_epoch_bump`. Shared fixture seeds `lease.json` and (optionally) `events.jsonl`.
2. `test_append_event_locked_tail_seek_handles_4kb_boundary` — append events until file exceeds 4 KB; verify subsequent appends still parse correct tail.

### Step 4: Subprocess race test (`tests/concurrency/test_locked_append_two_tab.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Two subprocesses, each loops N append calls reading current tail+epoch just before each call. Use spawn + `multiprocessing.Barrier` (pattern from `tests/spikes/test_flock_apfs.py:98`).
2. **Assert**: total events = 2×N; `verify_chain` ok; no partial lines; events.jsonl monotone in size.
3. Run 100 rounds — STOP-LINE.

## Phase 2: Session, Identity, Config, Lease

### Step 5: Lift existing ULID (`astrid/core/session/ulid.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Re-export** `generate_ulid` from `astrid/threads/ids.py` (correctness-4):
   ```python
   from astrid.threads.ids import generate_ulid, is_ulid
   __all__ = ["generate_ulid", "is_ulid"]
   ```
   When `astrid/threads/` is retired in a later sprint, the implementation gets moved here without changing call sites. Add a one-line comment noting this transitional arrangement.
2. **Test** `tests/session/test_ulid.py` is a thin import smoke (the underlying suite at `tests/test_threads_ids.py` already exercises the generator).

### Step 6: Paths, model, lease (`session/paths.py`, `model.py`, `lease.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`paths.py`**: `astrid_home()`, `sessions_dir()`, `session_path(id)`, `identity_path()`, `user_config_path()`, `workspace_config_path(cwd)`. Honour `ASTRID_HOME` env var (default `Path.home() / ".astrid"`).
2. **`model.py`**: `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Session(id, project, timeline, run_id, agent_id, attached_at, last_used_at, role: Literal["writer", "reader"])`. `from_json(path)` / `to_json(path)` atomic via tmp+os.replace. `timeline` and `run_id` may be `None`.
3. **`lease.py`**:
   - `lease_path(run_dir)`, `attached_session_path(run_dir)`.
   - `read_lease(run_dir) -> dict`. Returns `{"writer_epoch": 0, "attached_session_id": None}` if missing.
   - `write_lease_init(run_dir, session_id)` — atomic; called by `astrid start`.
   - `bump_epoch_and_swap_session(run_dir, *, new_session_id, prev_session_id, reason)` — acquires `flock(LOCK_EX)` on `events.jsonl`, re-reads current epoch + tail, atomically writes new lease (`writer_epoch += 1`, `attached_session_id = new_session_id`) via tmp+replace, then calls the internal `append_event_locked` body (already holding the same lock) to append the `takeover` event with `{prev_session, new_session, prev_epoch, new_epoch}`, fsyncs, releases. Returns `(prev_epoch, new_epoch)`.
   - `release_writer_lease(run_dir)` — under flock, sets `attached_session_id=None` (used by `consume_inbox_entry` and `cmd_abort`).
4. **Tests** at `tests/session/test_lease.py` and `tests/session/test_session_model.py`: round-trip, atomic swap, concurrent takeover → one wins.

### Step 7: Project current-run pointer (`astrid/core/project/current_run.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **New file** `astrid/core/project/current_run.py`:
   - `current_run_path(slug, *, root=None) -> Path` (= `project_dir(slug)/current_run.json`).
   - `read_current_run(slug, *, root=None) -> str | None` — returns run_id or None.
   - `write_current_run(slug, run_id, *, root=None)` — atomic tmp+replace.
   - `clear_current_run(slug, *, root=None)` — unlink.
2. **Schema**: `{"run_id": "<ulid>"}` — single field, no plan_hash.
3. **Tests** at `tests/test_current_run.py`: round-trip + idempotent clear.

### Step 8: Binding, identity, config, discovery, constants
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`binding.py`**: `resolve_current_session()`, `is_writer_for(session, run_dir)`, `current_run_dir(session, *, projects_root)`. Custom `SessionNotFoundError`.
2. **`identity.py`**: `Identity(agent_id, created_at)`, `read_identity`, `write_identity(agent_id)`, `bootstrap_identity(*, prompt=input)` (slug `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,63}$`). Prompt injectable.
3. **`config.py`**: `load_user_config`, `load_workspace_config(cwd)`, `resolve_default_project(cwd)` (workspace overrides user). Neither auto-attaches.
4. **`discovery.py`**: `discover_projects(projects_root=None) -> list[ProjectSummary]` enumerates `<root>/<slug>/project.json`, sorted by mtime desc.
5. **`constants.py`**: `STUCK_NO_EVENT_SECONDS = 60`, `STUCK_SESSION_MTIME_SECONDS = 300`.
6. **Tests** at `tests/session/test_binding.py`, `test_identity_bootstrap.py`, `test_config.py`, `test_discovery.py`.

### Step 9: WriterContext (`astrid/core/session/writer.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Implement** `class WriterContext` and helper `writer_context(session: Session) -> WriterContext`:
   ```python
   with writer_context(session) as wctx:
       wctx.append(event_dict)          # routes through append_event_locked
   ```
   - At entry: reads `lease.writer_epoch` once → stores as `expected_writer_epoch`.
   - `append(event)`: re-reads tail-hash from disk just-before-call (cheap with 4 KB seek), calls `append_event_locked(run_dir, event, expected_writer_epoch=self.epoch, expected_prev_hash=tail)`.
   - On `StaleTailError`/`StaleEpochError`: bubbles up. Caller catches and exits non-zero with clean message.
   - **No retry inside WriterContext** — single-attempt CAS is the contract; agent retries by re-running the verb (which re-reads epoch + tail fresh).
2. **Test** at `tests/session/test_writer_context.py`: happy path + stale-epoch surfacing.

## Phase 3: CLI Surface — Status, Attach, Sessions, Gate

### Step 10: Session CLI verbs (`astrid/core/session/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`cmd_attach(args)`** — args `<project>`, `--timeline`, `--session <id>`, `--as agent:<id>`. Bootstrap identity if missing. If `--session`: resume (re-print export). Else mint ULID, build Session. **Current-run discovery rule (FLAG-002):** read `<project>/current_run.json`; if present and `runs/<run_id>/lease.json.attached_session_id` is set → set `role=reader`, print takeover hint; if present and unowned → set `role=writer`, do NOT yet acquire the lease (lease is acquired by `astrid start` or by an explicit takeover); if absent → `run_id=None`, `role=writer` (no run yet, ready for `astrid start`). Print `export ASTRID_SESSION_ID=<id>`.
2. **`cmd_sessions_ls`** — list all sessions with id/project/timeline/run/role/last_used.
3. **`cmd_sessions_detach [<id>]`** — remove session file. Does not touch lease (writer's next verb fails clean).
4. **`cmd_sessions_takeover <run-id|session-id>`** — resolve to (project, run); call `lease.bump_epoch_and_swap_session`. If target is "warm" (lease mtime < `STUCK_NO_EVENT_SECONDS`), require `--force`. Print `prev_epoch → new_epoch`.
5. **`cmd_status`** — branching:
   - Identity missing → `bootstrap_identity()`, continue.
   - `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` unset → list discoverable projects + defaults from config + spelled-out `astrid attach <slug>`.
   - Bound → load Session; if `run_id`: read events.jsonl tail-5, inbox count (`runs/<id>/inbox/*.json` excluding `.consumed`/`.rejected`), current step from latest dispatched event, role from lease. **Literal breadcrumb output template** (criteria_quality-3):
     ```
     session  <id>
     agent    <id>
     project  <slug>
     timeline <slug-or-none>
     run      <id-or-none>
     step     <name-or-none>
     role     <writer|reader>
     inbox    <n>
     recent events:
       - <ts> <kind>
       - ...
     ```
6. **Tests** at `tests/session/test_session_attach_detach.py`, `test_session_resume.py`, `test_session_takeover.py`, `test_first_run_bootstrap.py`, `test_status_breadcrumb.py` — assert via exact template strings.

### Step 11: CLI gate (`astrid/pipeline.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Modify** `pipeline.main()`. After the entrypoint nudge, before `_dispatch`:
   - Parse first non-flag token as verb (with sub-verb for `projects`/`sessions`).
   - **Allowlist (brief-canonical, issue_hints-1 / issue_hints-3):** `attach`, `status`, `projects ls`, `projects create`, `sessions ls`, `sessions takeover`, `sessions detach` (added explicitly with note: brief omits but logically required), `init`, `-h`, `--help`. **`doctor` and `setup` removed from allowlist** (require session like other verbs).
   - **`author test` special case (callers-3):** when invoked with explicit `--project`, bypasses the session gate (developer/CI path). Without `--project`, requires session.
   - Non-allowlisted + `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` unset or file missing → stderr `no session bound — run \`astrid attach <project>\``, exit 2.
2. **Add** dispatch entries for `attach`, `sessions`. **Remove** `thread` dispatch at `pipeline.py:123-126` and `from .threads import cli as thread_cli` at line 124.
3. **Verb-internal enforcement (callers-1):** the gate is at the entrypoint only. Verb functions (`cmd_start`, `cmd_next`, `cmd_ack`, `cmd_abort`, etc.) now take a `session: Session` argument (resolved by `main()` and passed in). Tests construct sessions in-process and pass them directly — they bypass the entrypoint gate by design, not by accident. Document this contract in the new session package docstring.
4. **Tests** at `tests/session/test_cli_gate.py`: parametrise gated vs allowlisted verbs.

## Phase 4: Migrate Callers Off active_run.json

### Step 12: Migrate all production sites (`astrid/core/task/`, `astrid/orchestrate/`)
**Scope:** Large
1. **Each mutating verb adopts the pattern**:
   ```python
   session = resolve_current_session()  # raises if missing (or comes in via arg from main)
   run_dir = project_dir(session.project) / "runs" / session.run_id
   with writer_context(session) as wctx:
       # ... existing verb body, replacing every append_event(events_path, ev)
       wctx.append(ev)
   ```
2. **Enumerated sites:**
   - `lifecycle.py:154` (cmd_start guard): replace `read_active_run(slug, root=…) is not None` with `read_current_run(slug, root=…) is not None`. Preserves "one active run per project" (FLAG-003).
   - `lifecycle.py:201`: replace `write_active_run(slug, run_id, plan_hash, …)` with two calls: `write_current_run(slug, run_id)` AND `lease.write_lease_init(run_dir, session.id)`. Session's `run_id` is set + session file rewritten.
   - `lifecycle.py:205, 255, 269, 336, 212, 296` (six append sites in lifecycle/lifecycle_ack/inbox): use WriterContext.
   - `lifecycle.py:246, 284, 418` and `lifecycle_ack.py:143` (read sites): replace `read_active_run` with `read_current_run` for the pointer, and `read_lease` for the writer fence. Plan-hash CAS that used to live in active_run.json moves to gate's checking the bound run via `current_run.json + lease.writer_epoch` (gate.py:519, see below).
   - `lifecycle.py:256` (clear on abort): `clear_current_run(slug)` + `release_writer_lease(run_dir)`.
   - `inbox.py:216` (clear on consume): `release_writer_lease(run_dir)` (run continues but writer slot is freed). `clear_current_run` is NOT called here — consume-then-abort vs consume-then-continue is the existing semantic.
   - `inbox.py:259` (callers-2): `gate_command` gains optional kwarg `writer_epoch: int | None = None`; when set, gate skips the lease re-read and uses this value. `consume_inbox_entry` captures `expected_writer_epoch` from the outer WriterContext and passes it through. **Inside `gate_command`, the actual `append_event_locked` call uses the threaded epoch.**
   - `gate.py:519` (plan_hash CAS): replace `read_active_run(slug)` with `read_current_run(slug) + read_lease(run_dir)`. The CAS becomes: `plan_hash` arg matches the plan-hash of the current run's events.jsonl `run_started` event (cheap: tail-seek + parse run_started → cache it on first read in `gate_command`).
   - `gate.py:551, 832, 848, 942, 963, 1105, 1136, 1146, 1155, 1164, 1195, 1205, 1215`: 13 `append_event` sites → wrap entire `gate_command` body in a WriterContext at function entry; replace each append with `wctx.append(ev)`.
   - `hook.py:_walk_cwd_ancestors` + `_scan_projects_root` + `cmd_hook_stop` (all_locations-1): **session-bound takes precedence** — if `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` is set, resolve via session (project = `session.project`, run = `session.run_id`), regardless of cwd. If unset, fall back to the existing cwd-ancestor walk for the legacy `Stop`-hook path that runs in a project subdirectory. **Drop the projects-root scan entirely** (it was a foot-gun that re-injected preamble for arbitrary projects). If both lookups fail, no-op silently as today.
   - `orchestrate/test_runner.py:113` (callers-3): the `astrid author test` CLI's `read_active_run(project_slug)` becomes `read_current_run(project_slug)`. Author CLI accepts `--project <slug>` and bypasses the session gate — developer/CI tool.
3. **Delete** `astrid/core/task/active_run.py`. Remove `clear_active_run / read_active_run / write_active_run` from `astrid/core/task/__init__.py` lines 3, 82, 103, 120.
4. **Final sweep**: `grep -r 'active_run' astrid/ tests/` — only docs, migration script, and possibly `current_run` (string substring) should remain. Any production importer remaining is a failure.

**Scope:** Medium
1. **Enumerated test files** (FLAG-009, scope-2):
   - `tests/test_lifecycle_start.py`, `test_lifecycle_abort.py`, `test_lifecycle_runs_ls.py`, `test_lifecycle_peek.py`, `test_lifecycle_ack.py`
   - `tests/test_task_kernel_e2e.py`, `test_task_kernel_dispatch.py`, `test_task_kernel_nested.py`, `test_task_kernel_attested.py`, `test_task_kernel_gate.py`

2. **Add shared fixture** at `tests/conftest.py` (or `tests/_helpers/session_fixtures.py`):

3. **Mechanical sweep**: each test that called `write_active_run(slug, run_id=…, plan_hash=…)` now calls the fixture and then `write_current_run(slug, run_id)` + `lease.write_lease_init(run_dir, session.id)`. Tests that asserted on `active_run.json` shape switch to asserting on `current_run.json` (+ lease.json where relevant). Tests that called verb functions with `--project` switch to passing the session explicitly.
4. **STOP-LINE on regression**: `pytest tests/test_task_kernel_*.py tests/test_lifecycle_*.py` must all pass.

### Step 14: Concurrency + takeover tests
**Scope:** Small
1. **Two-tab race for a real verb** at `tests/concurrency/test_locked_append_two_tab.py` (extending Step 4): race `astrid ack <step> --decision approve` from two subprocesses; assert exactly-one-winner per round across 100 rounds.
2. **Takeover-during-append** at `tests/concurrency/test_takeover_atomicity.py`: appender loop vs takeover worker. Outcome: (a) appender wins → takeover sees stale tail and retries (returns success after retry), or (b) takeover wins → appender raises `StaleEpochError`. Assert via `verify_chain` + event-kind counts.

## Execution Order
1. Locked-append + exceptions (Phase 1) before anything else.
2. Race tests green at 100 rounds (Step 4) — STOP-LINE before Phase 2.
3. Session model + lease + current_run pointer (Phase 2 / Step 7) before CLI verbs.
4. WriterContext (Step 9) before migrating callers.
5. CLI verbs + gate (Phase 3) — gate flips on after verbs exist.
6. Migrate all 20 append sites + 7 read sites + 18 test files (Phase 4).
7. Retire `astrid thread` CLI verb (Phase 5) — minimal scope; threads/ stays.
8. Schema sentinel (Phase 6) and migration scripts (Phase 7) — parallel.
9. Docs sweep (Phase 8) last.
10. Validation (Phase 9).

2. `test_locked_append_two_tab.py` (race) — apex under contention. STOP-LINE.

  "changes_summary": "v2 contracts the threads-removal scope (only the `astrid thread` user-facing CLI verb is removed; `astrid/threads/` stays as an internal library still used by orchestrator/executor runners and 5 pack run.py files \u2014 full removal deferred to a later sprint, resolving FLAG-001/008/scope-1/scope-3 and the hype-regression risk). Replaces the deleted `<project>/active_run.json` with `<project>/current_run.json` (pure pointer, no plan_hash) plus `runs/<id>/lease.json` (writer fence) \u2014 preserves the \"one active run per project\" invariant (FLAG-002/003/correctness-1/correctness-2). Adds tail-seek 4KB read to `append_event_locked` (correctness-5), reuses existing `astrid/threads/ids.py:generate_ulid` via a thin re-export module instead of re-implementing (correctness-4), and makes `append_event` legacy wrapper explicitly test/migration-only with `WriterContext` mandated in production (correctness-3, criteria_quality-1). Adds Step 13 enumerating all 18 active_run test files plus a shared `attached_session` fixture (FLAG-009/scope-2). Specifies stop-hook session-precedence and drops the projects-root scan (all_locations-1); historical AGENT.md files left alone (all_locations-2); adds docs/HOOKS.md + docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md to the doc sweep (all_locations-3). Verb functions take a `session` argument so tests bypass the gate by design (callers-1); `gate_command` gains a `writer_epoch` kwarg so inbox re-entry threads the outer epoch through (callers-2); `astrid author test --project` bypasses the session gate as a developer/CI path (callers-3). Narrows CLI allowlist to brief-canonical set plus `sessions detach` (issue_hints-1/3); removes `doctor`/`setup` from allowlist. Variant-selection migration becomes a no-op stderr audit since threads stay in place (issue_hints-2). Tightens `default_timeline_id` validation to slug-typed (criteria_quality-2). Adds literal breadcrumb output template for status test (criteria_quality-3). Adds new test file `test_current_run.py` + new module `astrid/core/project/current_run.py`.",
  "flags_addressed": [
    {
      "id": "FLAG-001",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Contracted threads removal: only the user-facing `astrid thread` CLI verb + dispatch + SKILL.md instruction are removed; `astrid/threads/` stays as an internal library that orchestrator/executor runners and packs continue to import. Full removal deferred to a later sprint as part of the runner rewrite."
    },
    {
      "id": "FLAG-002",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Defined `<project>/current_run.json` as the project-level current-run pointer (replaces active_run.json's discovery role) with the minimal `{run_id}` contract. cmd_attach reads it deterministically; no ambiguity over multiple historical runs."
    },
    {
      "id": "FLAG-003",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Plan now explicitly states the 'one active run per project' invariant is preserved: cmd_start's guard switches from `read_active_run` to `read_current_run` and rejects if non-null."
    },
    {
      "id": "FLAG-008",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Hype regression risk neutralized by the scope contraction: astrid/threads/ stays in place, so orchestrator/runner.py:148/240 thread_wrapper calls keep working unchanged."
    },
    {
      "id": "FLAG-009",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Added Step 13 that enumerates all 18 active_run test files and prescribes a shared `attached_session` fixture in tests/conftest.py for the mechanical sweep."
    },
    {
      "id": "issue_hints-1",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Narrowed allowlist to brief-canonical set: {attach, status, projects ls/create, sessions ls/takeover/detach, init, -h, --help}. Removed doctor and setup; `sessions detach` retained with explicit note that brief is internally inconsistent on this point."
    },
    {
      "id": "issue_hints-2",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Variant-selection migration becomes a no-op stderr audit (threads/ stays in place, so the variant sidecar machinery in packs is untouched and no data is lost)."
    },
    {
      "id": "issue_hints-3",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Plan now explicitly notes the brief lists `sessions detach` as a deliverable but omits it from the allowlist; allowlisting detach is called out as a documented deviation."
    },
    {
      "id": "correctness-1",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "cmd_attach now reads `<project>/current_run.json` for deterministic resolution. If that file is absent, attach succeeds with run_id=None ready for cmd_start. Eliminates the 'multiple unowned runs' ambiguity."
    },
    {
      "id": "correctness-2",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Same as FLAG-003: cmd_start guard now reads current_run.json and preserves the invariant explicitly."
    },
    {
      "id": "correctness-3",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Plan now explicitly documents that the legacy `append_event` wrapper is test/migration-only \u2014 it raises StaleTail/StaleEpoch with no retry, and production code MUST use WriterContext.     },
    {
      "id": "correctness-4",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "astrid/core/session/ulid.py becomes a thin re-export of astrid/threads/ids.py:generate_ulid (preserving within-ms monotonicity via the existing _LAST_MS/_LAST_RANDOM counter). No re-implementation; when threads/ is retired later, the underlying impl gets relocated without touching call sites."
    },
    {
      "id": "correctness-5",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "append_event_locked now reads only the last 4KB of events.jsonl (with a 64KB expansion fallback for oversized single lines), instead of seeking to 0 and reading the whole file. Tail-hash extraction is O(1) in file size."
    },
    {

    "id": "FLAG-001",
    "concern": "Threads-subsystem removal scope: Plan accounts for pipeline.py:123-126 dispatch, 13 test files, and the astrid/threads/ directory. But astrid/threads/ is imported by 10 production modules \u2014 astrid/core/{orchestrator,executor}/runner.py wrap every invocation through thread_wrapper.begin_*/finalize_*/subprocess_env; astrid/packs/builtin/{generate_image,logo_ideas,iteration_video}/run.py and astrid/packs/iteration/{prepare,assemble}/run.py use ThreadIndexStore/write_sidecar/SCHEMA_VERSION; astrid/core/project/run.py uses generate_run_id; astrid/core/{executor,orchestrator}/cli.py use ThreadIndexStore. Clean removal is a multi-day rewrite hidden behind one plan bullet.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-002",
    "concern": "Binding-model gap: removing <project>/active_run.json without a project-level pointer to 'the current run' leaves cmd_attach <project> and the migration script unable to determine which runs/<ulid>/ is current when a project has multiple historical runs. Plan implies first-attach picks 'the unowned run' but defines neither the discovery rule nor a fallback when several runs are unowned.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-003",
    "concern": "Invariant regression: current code enforces 'one active run per project' via cmd_start's read_active_run-is-None check (lifecycle.py:154). After active_run.json removal, two attached sessions can each call `astrid start` and create independent runs in the same project. Plan does not state whether this is intentional \u2014 silent behavior regression hidden behind a refactor.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-004",
    "concern": "ULID generator duplication: astrid/threads/ids.py already implements a 26-char Crockford ULID with monotonic-within-millisecond guarantees via counter state. Plan vendors a fresh 'time_ms + urandom(10)' generator that may regress on within-ms monotonicity unless counter state is added. Right move is to lift the existing module into astrid/core/session/ulid.py.",
    "status": "open",
    "severity": "minor"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-005",
    "concern": "CLI-gate enforcement boundary: gate lives in pipeline.py:main() per Phase 3 Step 9, but existing tests invoke verbs directly via cmd_next(['--project', slug]). Plan does not specify whether the gate is replicated inside each verb.",
    "status": "open",
    "severity": "minor"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-006",
    "concern": "Inbox callers of gate_command: inbox.consume_inbox_entry (inbox.py:259) synthesizes an ack and re-invokes gate_command from inside `astrid next`. Plan does not specify whether the synthesized re-entry inherits the outer session's epoch/tail or re-reads them. Re-reading in a different lock acquisition can cause spurious StaleEpochError under takeover-during-consume.",
    "status": "open",
    "severity": "minor"
  },
  {
    "id": "FLAG-007",
    "concern": "append_event legacy wrapper retry semantics: wrapper reads epoch+tail outside the lock then calls append_event_locked. Under concurrent writes it raises StaleTailError to legacy callers; no retry. Either the wrapper needs a bounded retry loop or every legacy caller must migrate to WriterContext.",
    "status": "open",
    "severity": "minor"
  },

        Debt guidance:
        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.
        - Prefix every new concern with a subsystem tag followed by a colon, for example `Timeout recovery: retry backoff remains brittle`.
        - When a concern is recurring debt that still needs to be flagged, prefix it with `Recurring debt:` after the subsystem tag, for example `Timeout recovery: Recurring debt: retry backoff remains brittle`.



        Your output template is at: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid/.megaplan/plans/sprint-1-sessions/critique_output.json
Read this file first — it contains 8 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

This is critique iteration 2. The template file includes prior findings with their status. Verify addressed flags were actually fixed, re-flag if inadequate, and check for new issues introduced by the revision.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: robust. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.
        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.

Either scan whole file (defeats perf claim) or store plan_hash in lease/current_run.

2. **FLAG-011 (significant, completeness):** WriterContext doesn't cover `record_dispatch_complete` (gate.py:1099, called from pipeline.py:59 after _dispatch returns), `record_step_attested`, `record_nested_entered`, `record_nested_exited`. Plan says "wrap gate_command body" but these helpers run outside that body. The "every site uses WriterContext" must-criterion can't actually be met.

3. **FLAG-012/013 (minor, correctness):** session.run_id drift when cmd_start happens after another tab attaches (no auto-rebind specified); cmd_attach's "present and unowned → role=writer" is a dead-end (cmd_start refuses, only takeover can claim).

4. **FLAG-014 (minor, correctness):** Inbox-abort regresses — v2 says only `release_writer_lease` whereas current code does full clear. Leaves current_run.json pointing at an aborted run.

5. **FLAG-015 (minor, perf):** `verify_chain` still called at gate.py:533 on every gate entry — O(n) on every verb undermines the tail-seek perf argument.

6. **FLAG-016/017/018 (minor):** 64KB tail-seek cap may be too small for feedback-bearing events; takeover retry unbounded; SKILL.md line 62 still references `<thread>` argument that pack iteration_video still uses.
